FT6 automatic stop after 100 completed strategy trades (2026-10-25)

- Implemented the independent integer setting `Maximum completed strategy trades (0 unlimited)`, default 100. Presets 1-6 do not overwrite it; saved Custom resolution remains once per run. Negative values report an error, request a silent pause when available, and block entries.
- The tick dispatcher reconciles retained history before intrabar/new-bar processing, including the history-not-ready branch, and checks again after processing. At `count >= limit`, it invokes the installed FT6 Pause API silently, emits one Journal diagnostic, and blocks later callbacks. It never closes a trade or removes history.
- Count source is installed FT6 `HistoryTotal` plus `OrderSelect(..., SELECT_BY_POS, MODE_HISTORY)`, counting unique closed `tp_Buy`/`tp_Sell` tickets filtered by effective Currency and MagicNumber. Deposits/credits, pending records, open trades and other symbols/magic are excluded. The filtered count is cached while HistoryTotal is unchanged and rebuilt after changes or strategy reset; the live-order selection cursor is restored after a scan.
- Installed API evidence: `C:\ForexTester6\Examples\Strategies\C++\StrategyInterfaceUnit.h` declares HistoryTotal, OrderSelect, OrderType, OrderTicket, OrderCloseTime, OrderSymbol, OrderMagicNumber and Pause. The interface record provides both `Pause` and `pPause`; its Pause wrapper returns without action if `pPause` is null. The implementation checks callback and context pointers, blocks entries and reports failure rather than claiming pause when the callback is unavailable.
- Exact selected synthetic results on the final build: `ft6_first_touch.exe trade_limit_stop` PASS (new-bar and intrabar boundaries); `trade_limit_history` PASS (filtering, duplicate tickets, unavailable history/Pause contexts); `trade_limit_controls` PASS; `direction_filtered_lifecycle` PASS; `ft6_preset_lifecycle.exe preset_lifecycle` PASS. No other harness cases, broad suites, historical backtests, optimization or parameter sweeps ran.
- Build: MSVC 2022 Community x86 Release via `vcvarsall.bat x86`, installed FT6 headers, canonical strategy `.cpp` and unchanged `.def`, `/O2 /MD`, `oleaut32.lib`. DLL and both selected harnesses built. PE machine `0x14c (x86)`; exports exactly match the six names in the `.def`: InitStrategy, DoneStrategy, ResetStrategy, GetSingleTick, ReplaceStr, IntrfProcsRec. Scoped `git diff --check` passed for the source, both harnesses and guide.
- Deployment: FT6 process check returned closed; no process was terminated. Installed `C:\ForexTester6\Strategies\RangeBreakoutPullbackStrategy.dll`, 64000 bytes, modified 2026-10-25 20:25:14 +10:00. Build and installed SHA-256 both `9CD89C3C95B13928C166D0447E54B7536CFC6B4BF42D9864ADB3AB16F503F07D`. Prior DLL SHA-256 `06DD7B050A09A2991827C68A817B78AE88E42DD91D4D87CCE5C582760937A67E`; recoverable copy is `.codex_tmp_ft6_auto_stop_20261025/RangeBreakoutPullbackStrategy.previous.dll`.

FT6 direction-filtered impulse lifecycle fix (2026-10-25)

- Root cause: TradeDirection suppressed opposite-direction direct seeds and provisional continuation in ProcessImpulseSetup. After a long was consumed, buys-only could retain its high through later bearish cycles (mirrored for sells-only), so new allowed-direction origins below/above that stale boundary remained blocked. This was a structural lifecycle lock, not evidence that a particular annual trade count is required.
- Correction: observe both directions using the existing causal candidate/parent lifecycle. Keep the first-touch order-direction guard; add an explicit confirmation-mode guard at the first valid PB1 resumption, before order submission, entry diagnostics or entry markers. Prohibited opportunities consume normally and cannot queue a later entry. Seed/parent diagnostics distinguish execution=structure-only from execution=direction-permitted. The guide now explains execution permission and both-direction structural tracking.
- Production paths reviewed: direct seeds, candidate retirement/restart, oversized and continuation evidence, promotion and chronological replay, parent ownership/consumption, first-touch and legacy entry paths, range entry and ResetStrategy. No new lifecycle state/helper was introduced. CanSeedImpulse (including both consumed-origin and consumed-extreme checks), candidate retirement/promotion/replay algorithms, parent ownership, frozen ATR/touch levels and rounding, no arming-tick fill, Ask/Bid execution, depth/expiry/invalidation/busy/history/risk/EMA/weekend gates, presets 0-6, saved Custom options, first-tick resolution, reset behavior, 0.01 lots, adaptive risk and frozen structural 2R/3R targets remain intact. Range logic, MT5 and Pine were not modified.
- Regression: added the named direction_filtered_lifecycle case to tests/ft6_first_touch.cpp. Chronological live ticks and closed bars enter through GetSingleTick/ProcessBar; real right-side bars confirm pivots. No eligible parent or consumed-state clear is injected. Buys-only and mirrored sells-only consume an initial allowed parent, observe/consume an execution-prohibited opposite cycle, then enter a fresh allowed cycle whose endpoint is inside the old consumed boundary, only on a future touch/resumption. Zero prohibited submissions, no prohibited entry messages/markers or delayed orders, old-origin/PB2 rejection, newly confirmed-origin-alone rejection, unresolved contained-child ownership, frozen setup ATR, passed-touch recovery and restart evidence clearing are asserted. Both-direction controls cover both first-touch and legacy confirmation modes.

Automatic Pepperstone risk-buffer fitting

- Superseded the manual buffer-entry design. The calculator has no editable risk-buffer control or browser buffer payload: it automatically derives `planned_buffer = min(PEPPERSTONE_TRADER_RISK_SLIPPAGE_BUFFER_POINTS, conservative_max)` with fallback preferred cap 50 and minimum automatic protection from `PEPPERSTONE_TRADER_SLIPPAGE_POINTS` (fallback 10). For the observed 43-point stop and AUD 9–12 band, the calculated cap and generated `RiskSlippageBufferPoints` are 14; `AutoFitRiskSlippageBuffer=true` is included in the generated set. Stops unable to retain the 10-point minimum remain fail-closed, show the 30-point conservative minimum stop, and require only widening the stop and recalculating.
- Trader v2.42 adds opt-in `AutoFitRiskSlippageBuffer=false` by default, preserving fixed-mode behavior for existing/manual sets. Auto-generated sets enable it. Trader first preserves ordinary risk within the existing AUD band, then uses bounded binary search between the configured minimum and generated cap to select the greatest exact live buffer that stays at or below `RiskAUD_Max`; it fails before token consumption/broker submission if no minimum buffer fits. The chosen value is returned through all risk callers, included in standard-market preflight logging, and included in desktop-market structured human-readable outcomes. No existing safety gate, token/replay rule, or order-submission rule was weakened.
- Initial single focused pytest invocation produced 2 passed and 1 failed because the new Trader test looked up nonexistent `TryStandardMarketOneShot`. Phase 7: enforce nominated minimum Net-R targets after costs and final sizing

Defect and invariant implemented

- Pepperstone automatic Net-R targets previously used only nominal rounded risk, allowing a legal undersized lot or retained slippage-buffer risk to produce less than the nominated target. The shared live and Backtest calculations now retain buffered worst-case all-in risk after volume adjustment and use `max(RiskAUD_Target, final rounded all-in risk, final buffered all-in risk)` as the Net-R basis. Thus AUD 10 at 2R requires at least AUD 20 net when final risk is lower, and at least AUD 22 when retained buffered risk is AUD 11.
- Both MQL5 AutoTP helpers validate profit after final TP normalisation with OrderCalcProfit, subtract configured round-trip commission once, move an undershooting target outward by bounded legal point increments, and fail if the floor cannot be established. Buffered risk above RiskAUD_Max is a hard failure before any order is sent. The corrected shared helper is used by standard market, standard limit/trendline, EMA, and Backtest paths; manual TP remains unchanged.
- OANDA calculator RR quoting now determines legal units before calculating the target floor, uses loss-side conversion for all-in loss and gain-side conversion for reward, rounds TP outward, checks the 120% cap, and returns internally consistent estimated loss/reward and Net-R fields. Calculator-metadata OANDA submissions perform one fresh quote/factor/unit/bracket validation before their sole broker request; legacy/manual payloads are unchanged.

The prior OANDA test verifies fixed/percentage AUD-10-equivalent floor handling, lower gain-side conversion, whole-unit sizing, outward target precision, 120% failure, one revalidated mocked submit, and one mocked OANDA request.

ATR scanner Phase 1 corrective follow-up: cancellation lifecycle race and executable UI coverage

Correction

- Cancellation now records the exact task under _refresh_guard before cancelling it. start_refresh returns an explicit cancellation_in_progress non-started response until the cancelled task has terminated and that same task has been finalised. Finalisation verifies task identity before changing active settings, progress, or last result; the marker is cleared for cancellation, natural completion, and unexpected worker exceptions. Duplicate Stop requests are safe and do not finalise competing task state.
- Browser Stop now clears the auto-refresh timer before requesting cancellation, blocks Run and timer callbacks while Stop is pending, deduplicates Stop actions, restarts the configured full auto-refresh countdown after confirmed cancellation, and polls authoritative status plus restores scheduling after cancellation failure. Settings/Save/Reset remain available.
- The static JavaScript token test was replaced with a deterministic Node/VM fake-DOM, fake-fetch, and fake-timer behaviour test. It executes disabled-auto initialization/callback suppression, manual Run payload, active control state, Save-only behaviour, pending Stop deduplication/blocking, stopped status, and enabled auto-refresh timer replacement plus manual:false callback payload.

Focused verification

ATR scanner Phase 1: cancellation, manual runs, and auto-refresh control

Root cause

- Scanner settings saves and resets directly started manual refreshes, and the shared worker queued a second refresh whenever settings changed mid-scan. The page also disabled all action controls, had no cancellation operation, always scheduled browser auto refresh, and rendered old snapshots using newly saved settings.

Behaviour implemented

- Added persisted, strictly validated auto_refresh_enabled (default true for legacy settings) alongside the existing validated 30–3600 second interval. The runtime settings JSON was not read, modified, staged, or committed by this work.
- Settings save/reset now only persist and return settings. Active workers retain their immutable start snapshot; queued-settings refresh state was removed.
- Added Run scan, Stop scan, accessible auto-refresh switch, and editable interval controls. Settings and save/reset stay usable while scanning; Run/Stop swap availability based on worker state.
- Added local-only POST /api/atr-scanner/cancel. It cancels and awaits the active asyncio task without holding its cleanup lock, preserves the last good result, publishes a stopped progress outcome, is idle-safe, and permits a new run immediately after cancellation.
- Automatic browser requests are scheduled only when enabled; disabled automatic requests are also rejected by the service. Initial automatic scanning remains only when enabled. Saved/stop states restart the enabled countdown from the full configured interval.
- Results and basis now render from the snapshot settings that produced them, with an explicit next-scan notice when saved settings differ.

Alert expiry/email, trade numbering/chart folders, funding totals/commission, and ZORAUSDT repair

Root causes and implementation

- Alert expiry did not exist end to end: the UI omitted it and neither monitor enforced it. The Alerts form now defaults to No expiry, converts browser-local date/time to UTC ISO-8601 expires_at, preserves it during edit/save/reload/backup/restore, displays No expiry/expiry/Expired, rejects malformed or past values, prevents re-enabling an expired alert, and excludes expired alerts from evaluation, re-arming, Telegram, and email while keeping them visible. Legacy alerts without expires_at remain non-expiring; fixed-price removal after firing is unchanged.
- Alerts previously had Telegram delivery only. Added the shared standard-library shared/alert_email.py SMTP transport, independent Telegram/email attempts for Bybit and OANDA custom price/move alerts and existing percentage-move alerts, per-channel readiness/test results, and backward-compatible /push-test behavior. Email messages include source, symbol, type, condition, current value, custom message when present, and UTC trigger time. No address, credential, or secret is committed or logged.
- Email configuration required when delivery is enabled: ALERT_EMAIL_ENABLED=true, ALERT_EMAIL_TO, ALERT_EMAIL_FROM, ALERT_SMTP_HOST, ALERT_SMTP_USERNAME, and ALERT_SMTP_PASSWORD; ALERT_SMTP_PORT defaults to 587 and ALERT_SMTP_USE_TLS defaults to true.
- Forward trade numbering/folder creation was absent from normal journal synchronization; the legacy backfill was not used. Canonical workbook logic now numbers only non-test row_type=trade rows, preserves all existing numbers, treats padded aliases as collisions, derives monotonic F/C sequences from workbook rows plus configured chart roots, uses open time then close time for the existing year/month hierarchy, creates folders under TRADING_JOURNAL_FOREX_ROOT/TRADING_JOURNAL_CRYPTO_ROOT, and performs deterministic allocation under the journal lock. Target Trade Number cells remain text and receive folder hyperlinks. Folder creation and workbook persistence are idempotent and atomic; failures cannot leave a workbook claiming an unlinked folder.
- The first real reconciliation validator failures were false positives caused by different openpyxl access paths materializing absent default-blank internal cells. Direct OOXML inspection proved AA1554, AA1555, AD1554, AD1555, AE1554, AE1555, AF1554, AF1555, AG1554, and AG1555 were absent and semantically empty/default before and after the targeted package patch. Presence is now considered equivalent only for a completely default blank cell with no value, formula, nondefault style, hyperlink, comment, merge significance, or other meaningful cell state. Meaningful differences still fail, and every non-target OOXML package part remains byte-protected.
- Open Bybit Demo/Live positions had no settled-funding aggregation. The service now identifies the current lifecycle from executions after the last flat state, preserves lifecycle through partial increases/reductions, distinguishes account/mode/symbol/currency/side/positionIdx, fetches paginated transaction logs in account/category batches with a short cache, applies the Bybit API sign convention (positive received, negative paid), and exposes Funding paid, Funding received, and positive-received Net funding columns. Retrieval or hedge attribution uncertainty produces a sanitized warning and an unknown dash, never a fabricated zero; order rows remain dashes.
- Crypto journal-import audit found one supported execution-history importer shared by Bybit Demo and Live; no Binance account-history importer exists. The Bybit CSV importer now reports trading fee total, funding paid, funding received, net funding, and funding cost separately. Commission is trading fees plus funding paid minus funding received. CSV Fees Paid uses positive cost/negative credit, unlike the transaction-log API. Net P/L remains gross P/L minus the combined Commission exactly once, preserving stable IDs, trailing-open deferral, legacy reconciliation, deduplication, and repeat-import idempotency. Diagnostics explicitly report that Bybit Demo/Live Commission includes funding.
- ZORAUSDT was not invalid. The metadata source remains the collection response from GET /fapi/v1/exchangeInfo, followed by exact PERPETUAL symbol selection. The prior public-request path exposed an opaque generic 502 and had no official-host transient failover. It now records only the safe endpoint, exception class, HTTP status, and Binance code/message; retries/failover occur only for connectivity, 429, and 5xx failures, never permanent 4xx failures. Filters are selected by filterType, so reordered entries work, and optional ticker/range/open-interest failures preserve partial specifications with warnings. The one post-fix live request on 2026-09-07 resolved ZORAUSDT as Binance USD-M PERPETUAL/TRADING, tick size 0.0000010, with no warnings; no regional restriction remained.

Trader EA Phase 1: one-shot named-trendline lifecycle and manual re-arm

- `Trader.mq5` now requires a positive `TrendlineArmGeneration` to arm a named trendline. The same unchanged name is manually re-armed only by increasing that generation.
- A terminal-global lifecycle key is stable across account server/login, symbol, magic number, strategy, and trendline name. It atomically reserves an arm before submission, preserves working/consumed state through EA/chart/terminal reloads, and fails closed on uncertain state.
- Accepted pending orders and their resulting positions are adopted on upgrade, then become consumed when absent; TP, SL, manual/broker closure, cancellation, deletion, expiry, rejection, and ambiguous acceptance cannot recreate an order. An active lifecycle blocks a re-arm from placing a concurrent second order.
- Lifecycle logging is transition-based (`armed`, `working`, `consumed`) and marks explicit subsequent arms as `event=manually_rearmed`; no tick-driven replacement of a working pending order occurs.

Trader EA Phase 1 correction: lifecycle tracking, exact ownership, and durable recovery

- A working tagged trendline pending order is now recalculated on the original new-bar cadence and amended in place with `CTrade::OrderModify` for its current trendline entry, SL, TP, and recorded expiry. It never creates a second pending intent; a failed amendment keeps the last confirmed order and stops automatic replacement.
- Trendline orders carry a compact lifecycle comment containing the stable account-server/login, symbol, magic, strategy, line fingerprint and generation. The durable record stores that comment, exact pending ticket, expiry, working generation, and highest blocked generation. Pending adoption requires the exact comment/ticket; filled position ownership is matched through the original order's `ORDER_POSITION_ID` and `POSITION_IDENTIFIER`. Same-symbol/same-magic non-lifecycle orders only block placement and are never adopted, amended, or cancelled.
- The durable record is a locked `FILE_COMMON` runtime file named from the lifecycle fingerprint. It is read before the terminal-global cache, survives terminal-global expiry, is flushed before the cache is updated, and causes fail-closed disarming on corruption, contradiction, read, or write failure. Runtime state is not checked in.
- A higher `TrendlineArmGeneration` supplied while the exact lifecycle is pending or positioned is recorded as blocked and logged once as `active_rearm_rejected`; it cannot queue after completion. The user must enter a later higher generation after the active work has finished. Unidentifiable pre-correction working cache records are migrated as consumed, not adopted, to prevent accidental ownership of an ordinary same-magic order.
- Source property and runtime `EA_VERSION` are both `2.33`.

Phase 4 of 11: Bybit-first crypto Instrument Lookup

- Approved baseline: `3fb212eed572dd012ea9e6c8784c51c75840b84dddb`. The final focused commit containing this summary is reported in the delivery because a commit cannot contain its own hash.
- Root cause: `_fetch_instrument_specs` routed all crypto preferences straight to Binance, while the existing Bybit resolver depended on a cached symbol universe, used an account-derived base URL, and converted upstream failures into apparent misses. The browser also omitted an explicit crypto preference and hid the actual market-data source.
- Crypto preferences (`bybit`, `crypto`, `perp`, `perpetual`) and non-FX automatic routing now query Bybit first through the existing canonical helpers. Exact-looking pairs use bounded category-priority instrument queries; bare bases use `baseCoin` queries with deterministic `USDT`, `USDC`, then `USD` preference; cached fuzzy/alias resolution remains secondary with at most one forced refresh pass. Exact matches retain the category actually found for ticker, turnover, range, and BTC-reference enrichment.
- Instrument Lookup uses only a validated production public Bybit origin derived from `BYBIT_PUBLIC_MARKET_BASE_URL`; the ATR scanner public-origin helper delegates to the same validation. No credentials or account selection are consulted. Validated `retCode=0` empty responses can establish absence, while transport/429/5xx, malformed shapes, nonzero retCodes, and incomplete category lookups raise a Bybit-specific error and never trigger Binance.
- Binance remains available for explicit `prefer=binance`, or as a labeled fallback only after definitive Bybit absence. Fallback responses retain `source=binance_usdm` and include `_source_notice`; the UI sends `prefer=bybit` for crypto, preserves `prefer=oanda` for FX, uses the returned public-spec symbol for the unchanged journal request/history, and displays Bybit/OANDA/Binance-fallback source text outside the ordinary metric grid. Optional Bybit ticker, turnover, range, and BTC-reference failures preserve identified Bybit specs with warnings.

Phase 5 of 11: canonical symbol resolution for saved alerts

- Approved baseline: `75328b2039026a45a938f6ba4087155f3d4369b0`. The final focused commit containing this summary is reported in the delivery because a commit cannot contain its own hash.
- Root cause: the shared alert resolver used an account-derived Bybit URL plus fuzzy cached-universe matching, the Bybit POST endpoint did not independently verify the submitted symbol before persistence, and monitor restore/startup paths could rewrite or delete legacy rows when symbol data was stale or unavailable.
- Linear alert resolution now uses the validated credential-free public Bybit origin and exact `/v5/market/instruments-info` metadata. Exact-looking symbols are queried directly; bare bases use `baseCoin`; only one active `Trading` `LinearPerpetual` with exact base/symbol and USDT quote/settlement is accepted. FX automatic routing remains OANDA, while absence/unsupported contracts produce a client error and transport/API/malformed-response failures remain distinct 502 errors. Alert persistence has no Binance fallback or fuzzy starts-with/contains path.

Legacy shorthand is migrated field-for-field only after successful exact verification; unavailable or unsupported rows remain unchanged and are displayed as unresolved. Non-strict restore and monitor startup no longer discard unresolved alerts.
- The browser requests `prefer=auto&scope=linear`, submits only the resolved symbol, treats the canonical alert returned by POST as display authority, shows `Resolved <input> to <canonical>` when changed, preserves form values on failure, and keeps FX routing and all existing alert settings. Monitor coercion is exact-only and canonical saved symbols remain the keys used by the existing price-trigger path, including fixed-price removal after firing.

Trader Controls correction: previous-result display

- Root cause: the window refresh displayed any instance-matching durable result file as the current result. Because the EA instance identity is intentionally durable across reattachments, an older command result could appear as a current `MARKET ORDER | blocked` outcome even while the fresh readiness line correctly showed the new session was ready.
- The window now records command UUIDs issued during its own lifetime and renders a durable result only when its UUID belongs to that session set. Before a command is issued it remains `Last command: none`; mismatched persisted results cannot replace a pending/current outcome. A genuine stale/future-timestamp result for a command issued by this window still renders normally and remains fail-closed. Connection, readiness, UUID, freshness, pending-command, replay, consumption, one-trade, and broker protections are unchanged.
- Focused verification: `tests/test_trader_control_window.py::test_window_only_displays_results_issued_in_its_current_session` passed (`1 passed` in 0.29s; two existing unwritable pytest-cache warnings). It covers a pre-window persisted blocked result remaining non-current, a current-session matching result rendering, and a later mismatch not replacing it. `git diff --check` passed with only line-ending notices.
- Changed only: `mt5-clone/trader_control_window.py`, `tests/test_trader_control_window.py`, and this summary. No MT5, Trader window, token, broker, order, or production-data operation occurred. Reopen Trader Controls after updating to begin a new display session.

Phase 2 of 7: one-shot Bounce Trader workers

- Bybit now tracks only its session-owned `orderLinkId`/order ID, treats Triggered/Filled/PartiallyFilled/positive execution as terminal, applies any available fill-based TP/SL completion once, latches terminal state, and returns from `main()`. Amend uncertainty cannot fall through to create, and non-executed terminal/unknown states stop without rearming.
- OANDA now tracks its own order ID/client ID, resolves PENDING/FILLED/TRIGGERED/fill-transaction/trade evidence directly rather than using pending-order disappearance, and returns terminally after execution or non-executed terminal/uncertain outcomes. Cancel/replacement first resolves the old order so a cancel/fill race cannot create a second entry.

Pepperstone replacement-account timeline isolation correction

- Confirmed static boundary missed by the prior eight Pepperstone tests: once a statement seed was selected, Trade Log rows and cashflow-ledger events were still grouped only by canonical `PEPPERSTONE DEMO`/`LIVE` label. A later identified old-account trade, withdrawal, or reset could therefore affect the selected replacement account.
- `_cashflow_row_to_ledger_event` now preserves supported Pepperstone identity evidence, including `account_identity`, metadata fingerprint, and stable `pepperstone_mt5:<fingerprint>:...` IDs; pending-cashflow deduplication also distinguishes fingerprints. When an identified Pepperstone statement account is selected, timeline trades and cashflow checkpoints are limited to that same fingerprint. ## Job 7A — journal decimal policy and feasibility (2026-10-08)

Completed the bounded source-path and presentation-feasibility audit. No production code or real workbook was changed. Proposed policy: minimum 2 decimal places; for nonzero magnitude below 1, `max(2, 1 - Decimal(abs(value)).adjusted())`; quantize magnitude with Decimal `ROUND_UP` and reapply the original sign (away from zero). Zero displays `0.00`; missing/nonfinite values remain blank/unavailable; exact values gain no increment; carry uses the decimal places selected from the original magnitude; generated text has no 12-place cap. Convert Excel percentage fractions to displayed percentage points exactly once. R, pp gaps, ordinary decimals, price, quantity, and non-balance currency amounts remain distinct displayed units. Currency/account balances are explicit exclusions; IDs/counts/dates/durations retain existing types.

The actual adaptive helpers were exercised in one temporary Python probe on the supplied values and signed/exact controls. In table order, Decimal policy displays were `0.00039%`, `4.69%`, `0.00000035%`, `4.69%`, `-4.69%`, `-0.00039%`, `0.00%`, and `4.68%`. Existing helper metadata selected `0.0000%`, `0.00%`, `0.0000000%`, `0.00%`, `0.00%`, `0.0000%`, `0.00%`, and `0.00%`; `adaptive_number_format(0.00038475834753)` returned `0.0000`. These are Decimal outputs and number-format metadata, not Excel-rendered observations.

The plan at `docs/journal_decimal_presentation_plan.md` maps Trade Log, STATS 1, STATS 2, SYMBOLS, P&L Calendar, period reports, inline source text, recommendation explanations/charts, and formula-cache preservation paths. The key feasibility limit is that Excel number formats round according to their decimal placeholders and cannot request away-from-zero rounding. True text-display rounding can preserve underlying values only in cells already rendered as text. Converting numeric/formula cells to text or altering formulas/caches conflicts with the preservation requirements. Recommended Job 7B approach is to use exact Decimal formatting for existing text surfaces and leave numeric/formula cells and balances unchanged, while explicitly recording the remaining numeric-cell directional-rounding limitation. User decision is still required on whether that preservation-first split is acceptable; a strict all-visible-cell guarantee requires relaxing a stated value/formula/layout constraint.

Official references reviewed: Python Decimal modes; Microsoft Excel custom number-format placeholders; separate Excel ROUNDUP/ROUND/ROUNDDOWN operations; custom-format availability and Excel numeric precision. No pytest, Excel, real workbook, resync, runtime data, account, service, MT5, browser, or Dropbox operation was performed. The user's completed live dashboard Exit acceptance is recorded; no repeat was attempted. Job 7 implementation remains pending.

Changed paths: `docs/journal_decimal_presentation_plan.md` and this summary. Job 7A has no implementation or workbook acceptance claim.

## Job 7E journal-import Git collision (2026-10-10)

- The reported import reached saved-workbook verification and then failed at `git commit` because `.git/index.lock` existed (`workbook_sync=442.516146s`, `verification=2.527381s`, `github_sync=0.981176s`, `rollback=33.804741s`). The watcher previously held only a status-dictionary lock and could start its own Git transaction while import owned the workbook reservation. This is a proven coordination gap; the historical index-lock owner is unproven. Current inspection found no `index.lock` or Git process. No local worker/import log with the failed command was available beyond the user-reported error and timings.
- Read-only inspection found one Bybit Demo BTCUSDT row with ID `bybit:demo:trade:BTCUSDT:cd7d7d60e40d420e` in both JSON and Trade Log row 12, still dated 2026-04-10 through 2026-07-10. The October repair is not visible locally. That observation does not establish complete rollback or remote restoration. Starting local and remote master both matched `bdc80a25806a585e3b6aa41851c581a76f361b93`, a later journal-state commit, and unrelated working changes were preserved.
- `render/master_service.py` now uses one reentrant thread gate plus an OS-held byte-range lock for the workbook transaction and full journal Git sequence. The lock sits in the checkout's `.git` directory, coordinates separate service processes, and is released in `finally`; the watcher defers through import verification/rollback without clearing its pending save. An import that verifies and publishes its workbook marks those exact bytes known, preventing a redundant watcher transaction. The existing Git allowlist, tracked deletions, ignored state backup, and `commit --only` remain intact.
- External `index.lock` failures at Git add/commit receive a bounded 15-second wait by default. The code leaves the lock untouched, rechecks HEAD before a retry, and reports persistent contention or changed HEAD as failure. No commit or push is blindly replayed. No-op and push paths verify the remote SHA before reporting synchronization. Watcher success/fingerprint state requires verified remote equality and unchanged workbook bytes; import treats an unverified Git result as failure and retains its existing rollback/remote-uncertainty reporting.
- Focused verification: one pytest process, 14 exact nodes, 14 passed in 32.30 pytest seconds / 34.58 runner seconds, no timeout. New nodes in `tests/test_master_service_master_journal_sync.py`: `test_manual_save_defers_during_import_verification_and_rollback`; `test_journal_transaction_os_lock_coordinates_separate_processes`; `test_journal_git_sync_serializes_two_callers_without_interleaving`; `test_journal_git_index_lock_recovers_then_reports_bounded_busy`. Directly affected existing nodes in that file: `test_github_sync_real_repo_ignores_state_backup_and_preserves_unrelated_changes`; `test_github_sync_real_repo_without_eligible_diff_is_successful_noop`; `test_manual_save_failed_sync_keeps_fingerprint_pending_then_retries_successfully`; `test_manual_save_watcher_startup_reconciles_existing_workbook_and_exposes_local_status`; `test_manual_save_scan_debounce_and_service_write_suppression`; `test_github_sync_stages_only_target_file`; `test_github_sync_stages_recommendation_assets_and_tracked_deletions`; `test_github_sync_stages_legacy_master_journal_deletion_when_tracked_and_missing`. Existing rollback nodes in `tests/test_master_service_trading_journal_crud.py`: `test_import_file_github_sync_exception_reports_remote_state_unknown`; `test_import_file_github_sync_failure_reports_remote_state_not_restored`. Streamed log: `.job7e_verify_20261010_01/pytest.log`. Touched Python files passed `py_compile`; the final scoped whitespace check passed.
- Verification used temporary workbooks and local bare Git remotes only. The tests demonstrate code/fixture behavior, not a live worker import or GitHub journal publication. Microsoft documents mode 7 as minimized without activation, and Tk documents `wm deiconify` on Windows as raising/focusing a window, which is why startup does not deiconify and then iconify.

FT6 ZERO-TRADE STARTUP / DISPATCH FIX - 26 October 2026

Confirmed defect and limits of the evidence:
- The reviewed production code resolved settings and then called strcmp(Currency, Symbol()) before SetCurrencyAndTimeframe(Currency, Timeframe). An unselected SDK context can therefore return a nonmatching/empty Symbol and terminate the callback before binding; a null Symbol return was unsafe. The binding return value was ignored.
- The installed authoritative SDK StrategyInterfaceUnit.h (lines 335-340 and 758) says binding enables Symbol, quotes, Point, OHLC, Time and Bars, and returns true on success. Production now resolves effective settings once, validates Currency, binds the resolved currency/timeframe, checks the real binding result, then validates the selected Symbol safely before any context-dependent reads. Bind failures block that callback and can recover on later callbacks.
- Before patching, the new stateful cold-start selector failed on the production pre-bind early return. Exact native assertion/output is retained in .codex_tmp_ft6_startup_20261026/cold_start_context.before.log: "FAIL: cold-start callback binds context before reading Symbol or bar APIs" after the preset initialization line, with no binding, history or bar-processing event.
- The user's screenshot confirms initialization settings and Test started only. It does not prove whether the SDK bound its context or which later gate blocked a trade. The binding defect is reproduced and fixed, but its causal role in the reported full zero-trade run remains unproven. No actual FT6 run or retained tick trace connects it to that run. No thresholds were loosened or blamed without measurements.

Runtime diagnostics:
- Initialization remains a settings report, not proof of binding or processing. New event diagnostics identify `RBP startup binding` (configured/selected symbol, effective timeframe, outcome/reason), `RBP startup history` (available/required bars and waiting/ready), `RBP startup first-closed-bar` (first processed closed bar/time), and `RBP progress` (actual processed bar count and pending/qualified/touch/stage).
- Repeated equivalent failures do not spam the Journal. History reports change on readiness transitions; progress prints after readiness and then at most once per 250 processed bars. Diagnostic state resets each run.
- The faithful cold-start fixture exercises missing Currency, false binding, null and empty selected Symbol, recovery, and reset cleanup. Its end-to-end preset-7 path uses 512 synthetic OHLC bars and 220 actual production callbacks, retaining RegimeLookback=200 and ATRLength=14. Production resolves preset 7, warms history/ATR/risk, confirms a pivot origin, retains a seed pending at 2.67 ATR displacement, qualifies on a later completed endpoint close above the frozen 20-bar range plus 0.10 ATR at 4.0 ATR displacement, prospectively arms, then accepts one executable synthetic Ask quote and consumes the parent. Repeated callbacks do not duplicate the entry.
- Fixture-only corrections: the SDK seam now supplies the actual SetCurrencyAndTimeframe context pointer; 210 warmup callbacks yield only 197 ATR-percentile observations, so the fixture uses 220; seed and qualifying endpoint are separate completed bars so no earlier same-bar path is mistaken for a prospective touch.
